The **Donetsk People's Republic** (**DPR**; [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language): Донецкая Народная Республика (ДНР), [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Russian): *Donetskaya Narodnaya Respublika (DNR)*, IPA: [\[dɐˈnʲetskəjə nɐˈrodnəjə rʲɪˈspublʲɪkə\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Russian)) is a disputed [republic of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republics_of_Russia) with a capital in [Donetsk](562951.md), established on an illegally [annexed](18950961.md) part of [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md) in eastern [Ukraine](31750.md).[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10] The DPR was created in 2014 when [Russian-backed paramilitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_people's_militias_in_Ukraine) took control of settlements in the province.[^cite_note-11][^cite_note-12] This started an [eight-year war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas) against Ukraine. The DPR declared itself to be an independent state, but is often described as a [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) of Russia during the conflict.[^cite_note-puppet1-13][^cite_note-puppet2-14][^cite_note-puppet3-15] It was [illegally annexed by Russia in 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts).

Following Ukraine's [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity) in 2014, Russia [occupied](71944314.md) and [annexed Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation) from Ukraine. In March 2014, [pro-Russian, counter-revolutionary protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_pro-Russian_unrest_in_Ukraine) began in southeastern Ukraine. In the last census, the population of [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md) was 57% [ethnic Ukrainian](145144.md) and 38% [ethnic Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Ukraine).[^cite_note-McFarland-16] Separatist protesters seized government buildings and proclaimed the Donetsk People's Republic (DPR) and [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) (LPR) as independent states. In April 2014, Russian paramilitaries took control of several towns in the region, starting the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas_\(2014%E2%80%932022\)), part of the wider [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md). Russia covertly supported the separatists with its own troops, tanks and artillery, preventing Ukraine from fully retaking the territory. Russian citizens came to dominate the DPR leadership. In 2018, Ukraine declared the territory to be [occupied by Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-17] The DPR received no [international recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic_and_the_Luhansk_People's_Republic) from any United Nations member state before 2022.

On 21 February 2022, Russia [recognised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Address_concerning_the_events_in_Ukraine) the DPR and LPR as sovereign states. Three days later, it launched a [full-scale invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), partially under the pretext of defending the republics. Russian forces captured more of Donetsk Oblast, which became part of the DPR. In September 2022, Russia proclaimed the [annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) of the DPR and other occupied territories, following [referendums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_annexation_referendums_in_Russian-occupied_Ukraine) widely described as [fraudulent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election#Sham_election). The [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) passed a [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/4) condemning the "attempted illegal annexation" and demanding that Russian forces withdraw.[^cite_note-18][^cite_note-bbc20221013-19]

The Head of DPR is [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin), and its parliament is the [People's Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Council_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic). As a breakaway state, the ideology of the DPR was shaped by [Russian imperialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_imperialism) [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_nationalism), [Orthodox fundamentalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalism),[^cite_note-20] and [neo-Sovietism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Sovietism). Russian [far-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruscism) groups played an important role among the separatists, especially at the beginning of the conflict.[^cite_note-21] Organizations such as the [UN Human Rights Office](22463.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) have reported [human rights abuses in the DPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_situation_during_the_war_in_Donbas), including internment, torture, extrajudicial killings, and [forced conscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilization_in_Donetsk_and_Luhansk_People's_Republics),[^cite_note-22] as well as political and media repression. The DPR People's Militia has also been held responsible for [war crimes](#War_crimes), among them the shooting down of [Malaysia Airlines Flight 17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia_Airlines_Flight_17), killing 298 civilians.[^cite_note-AP_MH17-23] Ukraine declared the DPR a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-terrorist1-24]

## History

### Background



Percentage of Russians in Ukraine by province in the last census (2001), showing Donetsk Oblast with 38%

The Donetsk People's Republic is in the historical [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) region of [eastern Ukraine](13065940.md). In the last census, the population of [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md) was 57% [ethnic Ukrainian](145144.md) and 38% [ethnic Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Ukraine).[^cite_note-McFarland-16] Meanwhile, 75% of people in Donetsk Oblast had Russian as their mother tongue, although most Ukrainian citizens spoke both Ukrainian and Russian.[^cite_note-25] Since Ukraine's independence from the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) in 1991, eastern and western Ukraine typically had different voting patterns.

In 2010, Donetsk native [Viktor Yanukovych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Yanukovych) was elected [President of Ukraine](20688479.md). Donetsk was the province most strongly supportive of Yanukovych and his [Party of Regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_Regions). In 2013, [Ukraine's parliament](844993.md) approved finalising the [European Union–Ukraine Association Agreement](39410190.md).[^cite_note-26] Russia put pressure on Ukraine to reject the agreement and [imposed economic sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_embargo_of_Ukrainian_goods) on the country.[^cite_note-27] In November 2013, president Yanukovych suddenly withdrew from signing the agreement,[^cite_note-28] choosing closer ties to Russia instead.

This coerced withdrawal sparked massive protests known as [Euromaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan), most of them in western Ukraine and the capital [Kyiv](585629.md). The protests led to the [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity). On 28 January 2014, [Ukraine's government resigned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Azarov_government#Fall). On 18–20 February, [more than 100 protesters were killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maidan_casualties) in clashes with [Berkut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkut_\(special_police_force\)) special riot police in Kyiv; most of them were shot by police snipers. Despite signing [an agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agreement_on_settlement_of_political_crisis_in_Ukraine), Yanukovych secretly fled. Ukraine's parliament then [voted to remove him](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Yanukovych#Removal_from_presidency) and hold new elections.[^cite_note-b713-29] On 27 February, an [interim government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Yatsenyuk_government) was established.

There were [protests against the revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_pro-Russian_unrest_in_Ukraine) in southeastern Ukraine, which were supported by Russia.[^cite_note-wil234-30] [Leaked e-mails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surkov_leaks) and [telephone calls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glazyev_tapes) later revealed that the Russian state had funded and organized the protests, mainly through Kremlin advisers [Vladislav Surkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladislav_Surkov) and [Sergey Glazyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Glazyev).[^cite_note-31] The region mostly consumed Russia-based media, which promoted the narrative that Ukraine's new government was an illegitimate "fascist junta" and that ethnic Russians were in imminent danger.[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-2014Lessons-33] In April 2014, an armed conflict began between pro-Russian paramilitaries and Ukrainian government forces.[^cite_note-\:0-34]

### Donetsk protests

See also: [Capture of Donetsk (2014)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capture_of_Donetsk_\(2014\))



Pro-Russian separatists taking down the Ukrainian flag and raising the Russian flag at the Donetsk regional government headquarters, 1 March 2014

Pro-Russian protesters occupied the [Donetsk Regional State Administration Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Regional_State_Administration_Building) on 1 March 2014. They declared the unelected [Pavel Gubarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Gubarev) as "people's governor" of Donetsk Oblast.[^cite_note-35] Gubarev was a Ukrainian-born pro-Russian activist who opposed the Ukrainian revolution.[^cite_note-march10afp-36][^cite_note-leader-37] He was a former member of the [neo-Nazi](54361.md) militant group [Russian National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Unity) (RNU) in 1999–2001 and former member of the [Nazbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bolshevism) [Progressive Socialist Party of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Socialist_Party_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39][^cite_note-kpgubnaz-40][^cite_note-tsn-gub-41] On 6 March 2014, the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) (SBU) removed the protesters.[^cite_note-42]

The first violent death during the Donbas unrest occurred on 13 March 2014.[^cite_note-43] A pro-Ukrainian demonstration in Donetsk was attacked by a larger pro-Russian group.[^cite_note-44] In the fighting, pro-Ukrainian activist Dmytro Cherniavsky[^cite_note-45] was stabbed to death, and 15 others were wounded and hospitalized.[^cite_note-46]

Pavel Gubarev was arrested on charges of separatism and illegal seizure of power. On 16 March, pro-Russian protesters demanding his release forced their way into the Donetsk prosecutor's office and the SBU office, smashing windows and tearing down the Ukrainian flag.[^cite_note-47] Gubarev was later released in a prisoner exchange.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-march10afp-36]



Pro-Russian protest in Donetsk, 6 April 2014. Pictured are flags of Russia, the [Donbas People's Militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas_People's_Militia), the Russian Empire, and the [Eurasian Youth Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Youth_Union).

On 6 April 2014, between 1,000 and 2,000 protesters[^cite_note-BBC100a6414-49] attended a rally in Donetsk calling for a [Crimea-style referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_of_the_Republic_of_Crimea) on independence from Ukraine. Hundreds of masked men stormed and seized weapons from the [Security Service](709222.md) buildings in the cities of [Donetsk](562951.md) and [Luhansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk).[^cite_note-Unnamed_War_Sloviansk-50] Protesters then [stormed and occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capture_of_Donetsk_\(2014\)) the [Donetsk regional government building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Regional_State_Administration_Building), breaking down doors and smashing windows. They raised the Russian flag and demanded the regional council call a referendum the next day on joining Russia.[^cite_note-BBC100a6414-49] Otherwise, they vowed to take control and dismiss the elected government.[^cite_note-NovDnUa1-51][^cite_note-UPravda1-52][^cite_note-53]

As the [Donetsk regional parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Oblast_Council) did not agree to their demand, on 7 April the unelected separatists gathered inside the RSA building and voted to declare an independent Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-BBCtha-54] According to the Russian [ITAR-TASS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITAR-TASS), the declaration was voted for by some lawmakers, while Ukrainian media said that neither [Donetsk City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_City_Council) nor other district councils delegated any representatives to the session.[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-DCCNI-56] The separatists announced that a referendum on whether [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md) should "join the [Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Federation)", would take place "no later than May 11th, 2014."[^cite_note-itartassapril7-57] Ukrainian media said that the proposed referendum had no status quo option.[^cite_note-kpapr10-58] The group's leaders appealed to Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) to send Russian peacekeeping forces to the region.[^cite_note-itartassapril7-57][^cite_note-59]



The occupied Donetsk RSA building on 4 May 2014

On the morning of 8 April, the 'Patriotic Forces of Donbas', a pro-Kyiv group that was formed on 15 March earlier that year by 13 pro-Kyiv NGOs, political parties and individuals,[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] issued a statement "cancelling" the other group's declaration of independence, citing complaints from locals.[^cite_note-storyover-62][^cite_note-gazend-63][^cite_note-ENDRD8414-64]

The [Donetsk Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Republic_\(organization\)) organisation continued to occupy the RSA and upheld all previous calls for a referendum and the release of their leader [Pavel Gubarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Gubarev).[^cite_note-proclUP8414-65][^cite_note-66] On 8 April, about a thousand supporters of the Donetsk Republic rallied in front of the RSA listening to speeches and to [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_the_Soviet_Union) and [Russian music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Russia).[^cite_note-67] Ukrainian media stated that a number of Russian citizens, including one leader of a far-right militant group, had also taken part in the events.[^cite_note-68]

### Beginning of armed conflict

Further information: [War in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas)



[Sloviansk](2533448.md) city council under the control of heavily armed men on 14 April 2014

Russia deployed 30,000–40,000 troops near Ukraine's eastern border in early April 2014.[^cite_note-Buildup2014-69]

&#x20;On 12 April 2014, a heavily-armed paramilitary unit crossed the border from Russia and took over [Sloviansk](2533448.md) and [Kramatorsk](2864798.md) in Donetsk Oblast.[^cite_note-Wynnyckyj_151-70][^cite_note-Twickel-71][^cite_note-Unnamed_War-72] They raided the police and [Security Service](709222.md) (SBU) buildings, seizing a large amount of weapons, which were given to local pro-Russian separatists.[^cite_note-Unnamed_War-72] The unit of more than fifty militants [wore no insignia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_green_men_\(Russo-Ukrainian_War\)) and had been sent from occupied Crimea via Russia.[^cite_note-Unnamed_War-72] They included Russian Armed Forces volunteers[^cite_note-Wynnyckyj_151-70] from Russia and Ukraine,[^cite_note-Twickel-71] commanded by former [GRU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spetsnaz_GRU) colonel [Igor 'Strelkov' Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Girkin).[^cite_note-Unnamed_War-72] He was a right-wing Russian nationalist associated with the [neo-imperialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_neo-imperialism) movement and had taken part in the Russian seizure of Crimea.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-galeotti-74]<sup>: 14–17 </sup> In an interview for Russian ultranationalist newspaper *[Zavtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zavtra)*, Strelkov said that this action sparked the war in eastern Ukraine:

> "I'm the one who pulled the trigger of this war. If our unit hadn't crossed the border, everything would have fizzled out, like in Kharkiv or Odesa".[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-Reuters_Strelkov-76][^cite_note-wil2399-77][^cite_note-78]



Russian commander Igor 'Strelkov' Girkin, who took responsibility for starting the Donbas war in April 2014

The first conflict deaths in the Donbas occurred on 13 April, when the Russian paramilitaries fired on Ukrainian SBU officers outside Sloviansk. Ukrainian officer Gennady Bilichenko[^cite_note-79] was killed and several wounded, while at least one Russian militant was killed.[^cite_note-Twickel-71][^cite_note-80]

In response, on 15 April, the interim Ukrainian government launched an "[Anti-Terrorist Operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-terrorist_Operation_Zone)" (ATO) in the Donbas. This marked the beginning of an [eighty-four day siege of Sloviansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Sloviansk).[^cite_note-galeotti-74]<sup>: 14–17 </sup> However, Ukrainian forces were poorly prepared, and the operation soon stalled.[^cite_note-IrregularArmy-81] Russian separatist commander Strelkov said that Ukrainian forces were "extremely cautious" at first, as they did not know how Russia would react.[^cite_note-Reuters_Strelkov-76]

On 17 April 2014, an agreement was signed in [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva) by representatives of Russia, Ukraine, the EU and the US.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] It called for a ceasefire in the Donbas, for all illegal armed groups to disarm, and for all illegally occupied buildings and public places to be vacated.[^cite_note-84] However, leaders of the Donetsk People's Republic ignored the agreement and vowed to continue their occupations until a referendum was accepted or the government in Kyiv resigned.[^cite_note-85]

The Donetsk Russian separatists carried out their first civilian killings in the Donbas on 17 April.[^cite_note-86] They kidnapped local councillor [Volodymyr Rybak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Ivanovych_Rybak) after he tried to re-raise the Ukrainian flag in [Horlivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horlivka). His body was dumped in a river along with those of [Maidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan) activists Yuri Popravko and Yuri Diakovskyi – all had been tortured and mutilated.[^cite_note-HRW_April_2014-87][^cite_note-OHCHR-killings-88][^cite_note-89] Russian paramilitaries kidnapped more than two dozen people in the first month of the war,[^cite_note-HRW_April_2014-87] including eight [OSCE observers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE_Special_Monitoring_Mission_to_Ukraine)[^cite_note-90] and journalists such as [Simon Ostrovsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Ostrovsky).[^cite_note-HRW_April_2014-87]



Russian paramilitaries in Sloviansk with a [BMD-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMD-2) [infantry fighting vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infantry_fighting_vehicle), 16 April 2014

The OSCE reported that all the main institutions of Donetsk city observed by the [Monitoring Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE_Special_Monitoring_Mission_to_Ukraine) seemed to be working normally as of 16 April.[^cite_note-91] On 22 April, separatists agreed to release the session hall of the building along with two floors to state officials.[^cite_note-release2-92] The ninth and tenth floors were later released on 24 April.[^cite_note-93] On the second day of the Republic, organisers decided to pour all of their alcohol out and announce a prohibition law after issues arose due to excessive drinking in the building.[^cite_note-vice12-94]

Ukrainian authorities released separatist leader Pavel Gubarev and two others in exchange for three people detained by the Donetsk Republic.[^cite_note-gubafree-95]

#### Public opinion polls

Polling during March–May 2014 found that about 41% of those polled in Donetsk Oblast wanted a united Ukraine with decentralized power; 38% wanted a federalized Ukraine; about 25% wanted the province to join Russia; 12% wanted all of Ukraine to join Russia; and 11% supported the *status quo*. Meanwhile, 72% opposed the occupations of government buildings and 18% supported them.[^cite_note-upkiis-96][^cite_note-97] In an August 2015 poll, with 6500 respondents from 19 cities of Donetsk Oblast, 29% supported the Donetsk People's Republic and 10% considered themselves to be Russian patriots.[^cite_note-98]

#### "Referendum" (2014)

Main article: [2014 Donbas status referendums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Donbas_status_referendums)



A billboard promoting a 'Yes' vote in the referendum on Donetsk independence

On 7 May 2014, Ukraine's security service (SBU) released an alleged audio recording of a phone call between a Donetsk separatist leader and Russian neo-Nazi [Alexander Barkashov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Barkashov).[^cite_note-vendik-99] In the call, the voice said to be Barkashov insisted on falsifying the results of the upcoming "referendum".[^cite_note-upwire-100] Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) asked the separatists to postpone the proposed referendum to create the necessary conditions for dialogue. Despite this, the Donetsk Republic group said they would still hold the referendum.[^cite_note-101]

The "referendum" was held on 11 May in the separatist-controlled parts of Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts.[^cite_note-102] The organizers claimed that 89% voted in favour of self-rule, with 10% against, on a turnout of nearly 75%. The results of the referendums were not officially recognized by any government;[^cite_note-103] Germany and the United States also stated that the referendums had "no democratic legitimacy",[^cite_note-ENsr-104] while the Russian government expressed respect for the results and urged a civilized implementation.[^cite_note-105]

On the day after the referendum, the "People's Soviet" of the DPR proclaimed Donetsk province to be a sovereign state with an indefinite border and asked Russia "to consider the issue of our republic's accession into the [Russian Federation](25391.md)". It also announced that it would not participate in the Ukrainian presidential election on 25 May.[^cite_note-106]

#### Government formation



Denis Pushilin in May 2014

The first full Government of the DPR was appointed on 16 May 2014.[^cite_note-URSDDPR-107]

The political leadership initially consisted of [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin), self-appointed as chairman of the government,[^cite_note-pushilin1-108][^cite_note-pushilin2-109] while [Igor Kakidzyanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Kakidzyanov) was named as the commander of the People's Army.[^cite_note-kakidzyanov-110] [Vyacheslav Ponomarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyacheslav_Ponomarev_\(public_figure\)) became the self-proclaimed mayor of the city of [Sloviansk](2533448.md).[^cite_note-111] [Alexander Borodai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Borodai), a Russian citizen claiming to be involved in the Russian annexation of Crimea, was appointed as prime minister, while [Igor Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Girkin) was made Defence Minister. Borodai had a past working for an openly anti-semitic and pro-fascist Russian newspaper *Zavtra* which had called for pogroms against Jews.[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113]

The DPR government consisted of several ministers who were previously Donetsk functionaries, a member of the [Makiivka](1388010.md) City Council, a former Donetsk [prosecutor](440936.md), a former member of the special police [Alpha Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpha_\(Security_Service_of_Ukraine\)), a member of the [Party of Regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_Regions) (who allegedly coordinated "[Titushky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titushky)" ([Viktor Yanukovych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Yanukovych) supporters) during [Euromaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan)) and [Russian citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_citizens).[^cite_note-URSDDPR-107] This government imposed martial law on 16 July.[^cite_note-KPma-114]

The Russian conservative [think tank](37101.md) [Izborsky Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izborsky_Club) drafted the first constitution and provided a first document, which has not been acted upon.[^cite_note-115]

&#x20;In a later interview, DPR leader Alexander Borodai said that the makeup of the separatist government revealed the Kremlin's direct involvement:

> "The prime minister was a Russian from Moscow, the first deputy PM, [Antyufeyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Antyufeyev), was similarly from Moscow, the minister of state security had the same background, the minister of interior affairs had the same background, the minister of defense exactly the same, the prosecutor general was similarly from Moscow ... so I had to find someone born in Donbas to take my place".[^cite_note-116]

Elections in the DPR and LPR were held on 2 November 2014, after the territories had boycotted the [2014 Ukrainian parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election) on 26 October.[^cite_note-DNR30y-117] The results were not recognised by any country.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119]

The DPR adopted a memorandum on 5 February 2015, declaring itself the successor to the [Donetsk–Krivoy Rog Soviet Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk%E2%80%93Krivoy_Rog_Soviet_Republic) and Bolshevik revolutionary [Fyodor Sergeyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fyodor_Sergeyev)—better known by his alias "Artyom"—as the country's founding father.[^cite_note-120]

#### Consequences

Main article: [Humanitarian situation during the war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_situation_during_the_war_in_Donbas)



A damaged building in [Lysychansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lysychansk), 4 August 2014

The [United Nations](31769.md) observed in May 2014 an "alarming deterioration" in human rights in territory held by insurgents affiliated with the Donetsk People's Republic and [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic).[^cite_note-121] The UN reported growing lawlessness in the region, documenting cases of targeted killings, torture, and abduction, primarily carried out by the forces of the Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-un-122] The UN also reported threats against, attacks on, and abductions of journalists and international observers, as well as beatings and attacks on supporters of Ukrainian unity.[^cite_note-un-122] Russia criticised these reports, and said that they were "politically motivated".[^cite_note-123]

A report by [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) in 2014 said "Anti-Kyiv forces in eastern Ukraine are abducting, attacking, and harassing people they suspect of supporting the Ukrainian government or consider undesirable...anti-Kyiv insurgents are using beatings and kidnappings to send the message that anyone who doesn't support them had better shut up or leave".[^cite_note-124] There were also multiple instances of beatings, abductions, and possible executions of local residents by Ukrainian troops,[^cite_note-125] such as [Oleh Lyashko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Lyashko)'s militia and the [Aidar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aidar_Battalion) [territorial defence battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_defence_battalion_\(Ukraine\)).[^cite_note-126] In August, Igor Druz, a senior advisor to pro-Russian insurgent commander [Igor Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Girkin), said that "On several occasions, in a state of emergency, we have carried out executions by shooting to prevent chaos. As a result, our troops, the ones who have pulled out of Sloviansk, are highly disciplined".[^cite_note-127] By the end of 2015, there were 79 places in the combined DPR and LPR territory where abducted civilians and prisoners of war were held.[^cite_note-128]

A report by the United Nations [Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) (OHCHR) released on 28 July 2014 said that at least 750 million US dollars' worth of damage has been done to property and infrastructure in Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] Human Rights Watch said that Ukrainian government forces, pro-government paramilitaries, and the insurgents had used unguided [Grad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grad_rocket) rockets in attacks on civilian areas, stating that "The use of indiscriminate rockets in populated areas violates international humanitarian law, or the laws of war, and may amount to war crimes".[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] *The New York Times* reported that the high rate of civilian deaths had "left the population in eastern Ukraine embittered toward Ukraine's pro-Western government", and that this sentiment helped to "spur recruitment" for the insurgents.[^cite_note-133]

As consequence of the conflict, large swathes of the Donbas region, on both sides of the "contact line", have become contaminated with landmines and other explosive remnants of war (ERW).[^cite_note-134] According to the UN [Humanitarian Coordinator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_Coordinator) in Ukraine, in 2020 Ukraine was one of the most mine-affected countries in the world, with nearly 1,200 mine/ERW casualties since the beginning of the conflict in 2014.[^cite_note-135] A report by [UNICEF](19698110.md) released in December 2019 said that 172 children had been injured or killed due to landmines and other explosives, over 750 educational facilities had been damaged or destroyed, and 430,000 children lived with psychological traumas associated with war.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137]

### Breakaway state in a static war (2015–2022)



Map of the Donetsk and Luhansk republics in late 2014

On 1 January 2015, the [Russian ruble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_ruble) went into official circulation with parallel circulation of the [Ukrainian hryvnia](722696.md) permitted until 1 September 2015, however, [taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxes) and [fees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fees) were to be paid in rubles only, and the [wages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wages) of employees at budget-receiving organisations were to be paid out in rubles as well.[^cite_note-138]

On 12 February 2015, the DPR and LPR leaders, [Alexander Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zakharchenko) and [Igor Plotnitsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Plotnitsky), signed the [Minsk II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_II) agreement.[^cite_note-OSCEfulltext-139] According to the agreement, amendments to the [Ukrainian constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_constitution) should be introduced, including "the key element of which is [decentralisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decentralisation)" and the holding of elections in the LPR and DPR within the lines of the [Minsk Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_Memorandum). In return, the rebel-held territory would be reintegrated into Ukraine.[^cite_note-OSCEfulltext-139][^cite_note-DT12FEBY-140] In an effort to stabilise the ceasefire in the region, particularly the disputed and strategically important town of [Debaltseve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debaltseve), Ukrainian president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) called for a UN-led peacekeeping operation in February 2015 to monitor compliance with the Minsk agreement.[^cite_note-141] The [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) did not ratify the changes in the constitution needed for the Minsk agreement.[^cite_note-Olsza31815K-142][^cite_note-dwimsr-143][^cite_note-SiNYT-144]

On 20 May 2015, the leadership of the [Federal State of Novorossiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novorossiya_\(confederation\)), a proposed confederation of the DPR and LPR, announced the termination of the confederation project.[^cite_note-utenrp-145][^cite_note-146]

On 15 June 2015, several hundred people protested in the centre of Donetsk against the presence of [BM-21 "Grad"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-21_Grad) launchers in a residential area. The launchers had been used to fire at Ukrainian positions, provoking return fire and causing civilian casualties.[^cite_note-147] A DPR leader said that its forces were indeed shelling from residential areas (mentioning school 41 specifically), but that "the punishment of the enemy is everyone's shared responsibility".[^cite_note-148]

Issuance of the first DPR passports in March 2016 by DPR leader [Alexander Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zakharchenko). Zakharchenko was assassinated in 2018.

[^cite_note-149]

On 2 July 2015, DPR leader [Aleksandr Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandr_Zakharchenko) ordered local elections to be held on 18 October 2015 "in accordance with the Minsk II agreements".[^cite_note-zak1810led-150] The [2015 Ukrainian local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Ukrainian_local_elections) were set for 25 October 2015.[^cite_note-151] This was condemned by Ukraine.[^cite_note-zak1810led-150]

On 4 September 2015, there was a sudden change in the DPR government, where [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin) replaced [Andrey Purgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Purgin) in the role of speaker of the People's Council and, in his first decision, fired Aleksey Aleksandrov, the council's chief of staff, Purgin's close ally. This happened in absence of Purgin and Aleksandrov who were held at the border between Russia and DPR, preventing their return to the republic. Aleksandrov was accused of "destructive activities" and an "attempt to illegally cross the border" by the republic's Ministry of Public Security. Russian and Ukrainian media commented on these events as yet another coup in the republic's authorities.[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153]

After a [Normandy four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Format) meeting in which the participants agreed that elections in territories controlled by DPR and LPR should be held according to Minsk II rules, both postponed their planned elections to 21 February 2016.[^cite_note-Reuters_6_October_2015-154] Vladimir Putin used his influence to reach this delay.[^cite_note-NFm2Oct15-155] The elections were then postponed to 20 April 2016 and again to 24 July 2016.[^cite_note-156] On 22 July the elections were again postponed to 6 November.[^cite_note-157]

In July 2016, over a thousand people, mainly small business owners, protested in Horlivka against corruption and taxes, which included charging customs fees on imported goods.[^cite_note-158]

On 2 October 2016, the DPR and LPR held primaries in were voters voted to nominate candidates for participation in the 6 November 2016 elections.[^cite_note-pD21016D-159] Ukraine denounced these primaries as illegal.[^cite_note-pD21016D-159] The DPR [finally held elections on 11 November 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Donbas_general_elections). These were described as "predetermined and without alternative candidates"[^cite_note-160] and not recognised externally.[^cite_note-161]

On 16 October 2016, a prominent Russian citizen and DPR military leader [Arsen Pavlov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsen_Pavlov) was killed by an [improvised explosive device](265112.md) in his Donetsk apartment's elevator.[^cite_note-162] Another DPR military commander, [Mikhail Tolstykh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Tolstykh), was killed by an explosion while working in his Donetsk office on 8 February 2017.[^cite_note-163] On 31 August 2018, Head and Prime Minister [Alexander Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zakharchenko) was killed in an explosion in a cafe in Donetsk.[^cite_note-164] After his death [Dmitry Trapeznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Trapeznikov) was appointed as head of the government until September 2019 when he was nominated mayor of [Elista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elista), capital of [Kalmyk Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalmykia) in Russia.[^cite_note-165] According to Ukrainian authorities, 50 Ukrainian soldiers were killed in clashes with Donbas separatists in 2020.[^cite_note-166]

In January 2021, the DPR and LPR stated in a "doctrine Russian Donbas" that they aimed to seize all of the territories of [Donetsk](562951.md) and [Luhansk Oblast](494904.md) under control by the Ukrainian government "in the near future".[^cite_note-7281527doctrine-167] The document did not specifically state the intention of DPR and LPR to be annexed by Russia.[^cite_note-7281527doctrine-167]

### Russian invasion of Ukraine (since 2022)

Main article: [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

See also: [Mobilization in Donetsk and Luhansk People's Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilization_in_Donetsk_and_Luhansk_People's_Republics)



Tanks of the DPR and Russian Army in the Donbas, 23 February 2022

The general mobilisation in the Donetsk People's Republic began on 19 February 2022; five days before the start of [Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine). Tens of thousands of local residents were [forcibly mobilised for the war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilization_in_Donetsk_and_Luhansk_People's_Republics). According to the Eastern Human Rights Group, as of mid-June, about 140,000 people were forcibly mobilised in the DPR and LPR, of which from 48,000 to 96,000 were sent to the front and the rest to logistics support.[^cite_note-\:8-168][^cite_note-\:9-169]

On 21 February 2022, [Russia recognised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Address_concerning_the_events_in_Ukraine) the independence of the DPR and LPR.[^cite_note-170] The next day, the [Federation Council of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_Council_of_Russia) authorised the use of military force, and Russian forces openly advanced into the separatist territories.[^cite_note-171] Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) declared that the Minsk agreements "no longer existed", and that Ukraine, not Russia, was to blame for their collapse.[^cite_note-fgfg56-172] A Russian military attack into Ukrainian government-controlled territory began on the morning of 24 February,[^cite_note-173] when Putin [announced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_conducting_a_special_military_operation) a "special military operation" to "[demilitarise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demilitarisation) and [denazify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denazification#Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)" Ukraine.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-Waxman_2022-175]

In the course of the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), around 55% of Donetsk Oblast came under the control of Russia and the DPR by June 2022.[^cite_note-176] In the south of Donetsk Oblast, the [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md) laid [siege to Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol) for almost three months.[^cite_note-177] According to Ukrainian sources, an estimated 22,000 civilians were killed[^cite_note-22,000_civilians_killed-178] and 20,000 to 50,000 were illegally deported to Russia by June 2022.[^cite_note-Radio_Liberty-179][^cite_note-Mariupol_residents_are_being_forced-180][^cite_note-181] A vehicle convoy of 82 [ethnic Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Greeks) was able to leave the city via a humanitarian corridor.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]



On the first day of the referendum, 23 September. Denis Pushilin and [Andrey Turchak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Turchak) of Putin's [United Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Russia) party

On 19 April 2022, a town hall assembly was reportedly organised in Russian-occupied [Rozivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozivka_Raion), where a majority of attendees (mainly seniors) voted by hand to join the Donetsk People's Republic. This came despite two hurdles: the [raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raion) was outside the borders claimed by the DPR, and the raion had not existed since 18 July 2020. The vote was claimed to be rigged, and organisers threatened anyone voting against it with arrest.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185]

On 21 May 2022, the town of [Oskil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oskil_\(village\)) in the [Kharkiv Oblast](494901.md) was declared part of the DPR.[^cite_note-186] The town was later recaptured by Ukrainian forces during the [Kharkiv Counteroffensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Kharkiv_counteroffensive).

[Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev), the former Russian president and as of July 2022 vice chairman of the Russian Security Council, in July 2022 shared a map of Ukraine where most of Ukraine, including DPR, had been absorbed by Russia.[^cite_note-187]

*[Der Spiegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Spiegel)* reported that forcibly recruited men from Donbas were used as [cannon fodder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannon_fodder). According to DPR officials, more than 3,000 were killed and over 13,000 wounded, "a casualty rate of 80 percent of the initial fighting force."[^cite_note-188] Human rights activists reported a huge – up to 30,000 people as of August 2022 – [death toll among mobilised recruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war) in clashes with the well-trained [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md).[^cite_note-\:8-168][^cite_note-\:9-169] On 16 August 2022, Vladimir Putin stated that "the objectives of this operation are clearly defined – ensuring the security of Russia and our citizens, protecting the residents of Donbass from genocide."[^cite_note-189]

#### Annexation by Russia

See also: [2022 annexation referendums in Russian-occupied Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_annexation_referendums_in_Russian-occupied_Ukraine) and [Russian annexation of Donetsk, Kherson, Luhansk and Zaporizhzhia oblasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts)

On 20 September 2022, the [People's Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Council_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic) of the Donetsk People's Republic scheduled a referendum on the [republic's entry into Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_Russia) as a [federal subject](701438.md) for 23–27 September.[^cite_note-190] It was widely described as a [sham referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election#Sham_election) by commentators and denounced by various countries. On 21 September, Russian president Putin announced a partial [mobilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_mobilization) in Russia. He said that "in order to protect our motherland, its sovereignty and territorial integrity, and to ensure the safety of our people and people in the liberated territories", he decided to declare a partial mobilisation.[^cite_note-191] On 30 September 2022, Russia's president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) announced the [annexation of the DPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) along with the Luhansk People's Republic and two other oblasts of Ukraine in an address to both houses of the [Russian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Assembly_\(Russia\)). On 12 October 2022, the United Nations General Assembly voted in [Resolution ES-11/4 to condemn the annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/4). The resolution received a vast majority of 143 countries in support of condemning Russia's annexation, 35 abstaining, and only 5 against condemning Russia's annexation.[^cite_note-bbc20221013-19]

Since annexation, Russia has intensified its [Russification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russification) campaign in the DPR in different aspects of daily life.[^cite_note-192] Russian authorities have insisted that [Ukrainian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) lessons are available among Russian-occupied territories, including in DPR. However, critics believe that these are only propaganda, and that Ukrainian is actually disappearing everywhere.[^cite_note-193] As a result, Ukrainian schools are in demand for children living in occupied territories, and there are proposals to establish Ukrainian schooling online for them.[^cite_note-194]

## Government and politics



Then-Chairman of the People's Council, [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin), speaks at a [Victory Day (9 May)](337670.md) rally in Donetsk in 2014.

In early April 2014, a Donetsk People's Council was formed out of protesters who occupied the building of the [Donetsk Oblast Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Oblast_Council) on 6 April 2014.[^cite_note-NovDnUa1-51][^cite_note-UPravda1-52][^cite_note-NovDnUa2-195] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* described the self-proclaimed state as [neo-Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Sovietism),[^cite_note-Kramer1-196] while [Al Jazeera](30204052.md) described it as [neo-Stalinist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Stalinism) and a "[totalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totalitarianism), North Korea-like statelet".[^cite_note-197] Administration proper in DPR territories was performed by those authorities which performed these functions prior to the war in Donbas.[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198] The DPR leadership has also appointed mayors.[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] Some sources described the "Donetsk People's Republic" during this period as a Russian [puppet government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state).[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203]

On 5 February 2020, Denis Pushilin unexpectedly appointed [Vladimir Pashkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Pashkov), a Russian citizen and former deputy governor of Russia's [Irkutsk Oblast](79477.md), as the chairman of the government.[^cite_note-204] This appointment was received in Ukraine as a demonstration of direct control over DPR by Russia.[^cite_note-205]

Several Russian officials were appointed to cabinet posts and prime ministership of the DPR in June and July 2022.[^cite_note-206]

### Head

The Head of the Donetsk People's Republic ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language): Глава Донецкой Народной Республики, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Russian): *Glava Donetskoy Narodnoy Respubliki*) is the highest office of the Donetsk People's Republic. The following persons have occupied the post:

- [Alexander Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zakharchenko) (1976–2018) 4 November 2014 – 31 August 2018 †. Political party: [Donetsk Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Republic_\(political_party\)).

  [^cite_note-207]

  [^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-208]

  &#x20;[Leaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surkov_leaks) emails suggest that Zakharchenko was recruited and installed by Russia.

  [^cite_note-209]

- [Dmitry Trapeznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Trapeznikov) (born 1981) *Acting* 31 August 2018 – 7 September 2018. Independent

  [^cite_note-210]

- [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin) (born 1981) 7 September 2018 – Incumbent (Acting: 7 September 2018 – 20 November 2018). Political party: Donetsk Republic, later [United Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Russia)

  [^cite_note-211]

### Legislature

Main article: [People's Council of the Donetsk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Council_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic)

The parliament of the Donetsk People's Republic is the [People's Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Council_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic)[^cite_note-BBCN8614-212] and has 100 deputies.[^cite_note-DNR30y-117]

### Administrative divisions



Administrative divisions of the DPR

On 6 April 2023, the DPR authorities announced a reorganization of [administrative divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Donetsk_People's_Republic?action=edit\&redlink=1) establishing 12 [cities of republic significance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_federal_subject_significance) and 18 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Russia). The organization is similar to the [administrative divisions of Donetsk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Donetsk_Oblast) before 2020, but [Marinka Raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marinka_Raion) is changed to [Kurakhovo Raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhove), and the number of cities decreased from 28 to 12.[^cite_note-АТУ-213][^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215]

The cities are as follows: [Gorlovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorlovka), [Debaltsevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debaltsevo), [Dokuchayevsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dokuchayevsk), [Donetsk](562951.md), [Yenakievo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yenakievo), [Ilovaisk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilovaisk), [Kramatorsk](2864798.md), [Makeevka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makeevka), [Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol), [Snezhnoye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snizhne), [Torez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torez), and [Khartsyzk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khartsyzk).[^cite_note-АТУ-213]

The districts are as follows: [Aleksandrovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandrivka_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), [Amvrosievka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amvrosiivka_Raion), [Artyomovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakhmut_Raion), [Velyka Novosyolka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velyka_Novosilka_Raion), [Volnovakha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volnovakha_Raion), [Volodarskoye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodarske_Raion), [Dobropolye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobropillia_Raion), [Konstantinovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostiantynivka_Raion), [Krasnoarmeysk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pokrovsk_Raion), [Krasny Liman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyman_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), [Kurakhovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhove_Raion?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Mangush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhush_Raion), [Novoazovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novoazovsk_Raion), [Slaviansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloviansk_Raion), [Starobeshevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starobesheve_Raion), [Telmanovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telmanove_Raion), [Shakhtarsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakhtarsk_Raion), and [Yasynuvata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasynuvata_Raion).[^cite_note-АТУ-213]
