Passport of the Donetsk People's Republic

### Passports and citizenship

In March 2016, the DPR began to issue passports[^cite_note-pdnr23316-216] despite a 2015 statement by Zakharchenko that, without at least partial recognition of DPR, local passports would be a "waste of resources".[^cite_note-pdnr23316-216] In November 2016 the DPR announced that all of its citizens had [dual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_citizenship) [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_citizenship)/Donetsk People's Republic citizenship.[^cite_note-217]

In June 2019, Russia started giving [Russian passports](8887086.md) to the inhabitants of the DPR and [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) under a simplified procedure allegedly on "humanitarian grounds" (such as enabling international travel for eastern Ukrainian residents whose passports have expired).[^cite_note-49207353Russianpassports-218] Since December 2019 [Ukrainian passports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_passport) are no longer considered a valid identifying document in the DPR, and [Ukrainian licence plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_registration_plates_of_Ukraine) have been declared illegal.[^cite_note-7March2020DonbasUW-219] Meanwhile, the previous favourable view of Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) in the DPR press was replaced with personal accusations of [genocide](12441.md) and "crimes against Donbas", and proposals of organising a tribunal against him [in absentia](582826.md).[^cite_note-7March2020DonbasUW-219] In March 2020 [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) was declared to be the only state language of the DPR;[^cite_note-2020/03/06/russkij-220] previously in its May 2014 constitution, the DPR had declared both Russian and [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) its official languages.[^cite_note-URSDDPR-107]

According to the [Ukrainian press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_press), by mid-2021, local residents received half a million Russian passports.[^cite_note-2524257300690UnitedRussia-221] Deputy [Kremlin Chief of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin_Chief_of_Staff) [Dmitry Kozak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Kozak) stated in a July 2021 interview with *[Politique internationale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politique_internationale)* that 470,000 local residents had received Russian passports; he added that "as soon as the situation in [Donbass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) is resolved "....The general procedure for granting citizenship will be restored.[^cite_note-7301163RFPKozak-222]

### Military

Main article: [Russian separatist forces in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas)



Banner of the Ministry of Defence

The Donbas People's Militia was formed by [Pavel Gubarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Gubarev), who was elected People's Governor of Donetsk Oblast and [Igor Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Girkin), appointed the Minister of Defence of the Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-WPost_Kharkiv-223] The People's Militia of the DPR ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language): [Вооружённые силы ДНР](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%92%D0%BE%D0%BE%D1%80%D1%83%D0%B6%D1%91%D0%BD%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B5%20%D1%81%D0%B8%D0%BB%D1%8B%20%D0%94%D0%9D%D0%A0)) comprise the [Russian separatist forces in the DPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas). On 10 January 2020 president of the non-recognised pro-Russian [Abkhazia](18933375.md) accused DPR of staging a coup in his country. DPR commander [Akhra Avidzba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhra_Avidzba) was commanding on the spot.[^cite_note-224] Unlike [South Ossetia](318381.md), Abkhazia had not then recognised DPR.[^cite_note-225]

The award Hero of the Donetsk People's Republic ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language): Герой Донецкой Народной Республики) was bestowed on

- [Joseph Kobzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Kobzon) – Russian singer

  [^cite_note-226]

- [Ramzan Kadyrov](647880.md) — Head of Chechen Republic

  [^cite_note-227]

### Problems of governance



Police in Donetsk wearing insignia related to the Donetsk People's Republic, 20 September 2014



DPR military parade in Donetsk, 9 May 2018

[OSCE monitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE_Special_Monitoring_Mission_to_Ukraine) met with the self-proclaimed mayor of [Sloviansk](2533448.md), Volodymyr Pavlenko, on 20 June 2014.[^cite_note-OSCE622-228] According to him, sewage systems in Sloviansk had collapsed, resulting in the release of least 10,000 litres of untreated sewage into the river [Sukhyi Torets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhyi_Torets), a tributary of the [Seversky Donets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seversky_Donets). He called this an "environmental catastrophe", and said that it had the potential to affect both Russia and Ukraine.[^cite_note-OSCE622-228]

As of May 2014, the [Ukrainian Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Government) was paying wages and pensions for the inhabitants of the DPR.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] The closing of bank branches led to problems in receiving these,[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] especially since the [National Bank of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Ukraine) ordered banks to suspend financial transactions in places which are not controlled by the Ukrainian authorities on 7 August 2014.[^cite_note-Oschadbank_HS?-235] Only the Oschadbank ([State Savings Bank of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Savings_Bank_of_Ukraine)) continued to function in territories controlled by the DPR, but it also closed its branches there on 1 December 2014.[^cite_note-Oschadbank_HS?-235][^cite_note-236] In response, tens of thousands of pensioners have registered their address as being in Ukrainian-controlled areas while still living in separatist-controlled areas, and must travel outside of separatist areas to collect their pensions on a monthly basis.[^cite_note-237]

In October 2014, the DPR announced the creation of its own central bank and tax office, obliging residents to register to the DPR and pay taxes to it.[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] Some local entrepreneurs refused to register.[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238]

According to the [National Security and Defense Council of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_and_Defense_Council_of_Ukraine) a number of local mutinies have taken place due to unpaid wages and pensions, the council claims that on 24 November 2014, the local "Women Resistance Battalion" presented to Zakharchenko an ultimatum to get out of Donetsk in two months.[^cite_note-239]

Since April 2015, the DPR has been issuing its own vehicle number plates.[^cite_note-240]

The [OSCE Special Monitoring Mission to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE_Special_Monitoring_Mission_to_Ukraine) reported that in the DPR, "parallel 'justice systems' have begun operating".[^cite_note-OSCE_SMM_241215-241] They found this new judiciary to be "non-transparent, subject to constant change, seriously under-resourced and, in many instances, completely non-functional".[^cite_note-OSCE_SMM_241215-241]

### Law and order

See also: [List of Ukrainian toponyms that were changed as part of decommunization in 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ukrainian_toponyms_that_were_changed_as_part_of_decommunization_in_2016)

The Ministry of Internal Affairs is the DPR's agency responsible for implementing law and order.[^cite_note-242]

In 2014, the DPR introduced the [death penalty](5902.md) for cases of treason, espionage, and assassination of political leaders. There had already been accusations of [extrajudicial execution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_execution).[^cite_note-243] After 2015 a number of DPR and LPR field commanders and other significant figures were killed or otherwise removed from power.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-deutsche-welle-245] This included [Cossack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cossack) commander Pavel Dryomov, commander of Private Military Company (*ЧВК*) [Dmitry Utkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Utkin) ("Wagner"), [Alexander Bednov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Bednov) ("Batman"), [Aleksey Mozgovoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksey_Mozgovoy), Yevgeny Ishchenko, [Andrei Purgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Purgin) and Dmitry Lyamin (the last two arrested).[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] In August 2016 [Igor Plotnitsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Plotnitsky), head of [LPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic), was seriously injured in a car bombing attack in [Luhansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk).[^cite_note-248] In September 2016 Evgeny Zhilin (Yevhen Zhylin), leader of the separatist "Oplot" unit, was killed in a restaurant near Moscow.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] In October 2016 military commander [Arseniy Pavlov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsen_Pavlov) ("Motorola") was killed by an [IED](265112.md) planted at his house.[^cite_note-251] In February 2017 [Mikhail Tolstykh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Tolstykh) ("Givi") was killed in his office with a rocket,[^cite_note-death-252] later reported to have been remotely launched by Ukrainian agents.[^cite_note-253] On 31 August 2018 DPR leader [Alexander Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zakharchenko) was killed by a bomb in a restaurant in [Donetsk](562951.md).[^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-208] The DPR and Russia blamed the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md); Ukraine rejected these accusations, stating that Zakharchenko's death was the result of civil strife in the DPR.[^cite_note-deutsche-welle-245]

In May 2015, Ukrainian president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) signed four laws concerning [decommunisation in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decommunisation_in_Ukraine). Various cities and many villages in Donbas [were renamed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ukrainian_toponyms_that_were_changed_as_part_of_de-communization_in_2016?action=edit\&redlink=1). The Ukrainian decommunisation laws were condemned by the DPR.[^cite_note-254]

In addition to Ukrainian [prisoners of war](25008.md) there are reports of "thousands" of prisoners who were arrested as part of internal fighting between various militant groups inside DPR.[^cite_note-255]

Political scientist Stephen Shenfield summarises the witness account of life in DPR as

- Political repressions on a level of [Stalin's terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Purge)

- Power and wealth concentrated in hands of an elite

- Industrial base destroyed by fighting, stolen, or moved to Russia

- Large unemployment rate due to the destruction of the industry, and former workers joining armed formations to get paid.

  [^cite_note-256]

In 2022, during the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), three soldiers of the [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md), [Aiden Aslin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aiden_Aslin), [Shaun Pinner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaun_Pinner), and [Brahim Saadoune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brahim_Saadoune), were sentenced to death. The DPR lifted the death penalty moratorium.[^cite_note-257]

### Ideology



Official DPR celebration for [Victory Day (9 May)](337670.md) 2014, showing the flags of the Donetsk People's Republic, the Soviet [Victory Banner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Banner), and the [Black-yellow-white flag of the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-yellow-white_flag_of_the_Russian_Empire).



Flags of three far-right separatist groups in Donbas: [Rusich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusich_Group), [Russian National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Unity_\(2000\)), and the [Russian Imperial Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Imperial_Movement).

Further information: [Ruscism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruscism)

According to a 2016 report by the [French Institute of International Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_fran%C3%A7ais_des_relations_internationales) (IFRI), [Russian imperialist nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_nationalism) has shaped the official ideology of the Donetsk and [Luhansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) People's Republics.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] According to [Marlène Laruelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marl%C3%A8ne_Laruelle), separatist ideologues in Donbas produced an ideology composed of three strands of [Russian nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_nationalism): [neo-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashism), [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalism), and [neo-Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_patriotism).[^cite_note-Averre-259]

Journalist Andrew Kramer observed that the DPR had institutionalized [nostalgia for the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nostalgia_for_the_Soviet_Union), and reintroduced some Soviet state policies, including the nationalisation of the mining industry and commercial agriculture.[^cite_note-Kramer1-196] The [flag of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_the_Soviet_Union) is commonly flown at official events, parades, and celebrations in the DPR.[^cite_note-Kramer1-196] However, an article in *[Dissent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissent_\(American_magazine\))* noted that "despite their [neo-Stalinist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Stalinist) paraphernalia, many of the Russian-speaking nationalists Russia supports in the Donbass are just as right-wing as their counterparts from the [Azov Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov_Battalion)".[^cite_note-260]

During the Donbas war, especially at the beginning, far-right groups played an important role on the pro-Russian side, arguably more so than on the Ukrainian side.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258][^cite_note-Averre-259] Members and former members of neo-Nazi group [Russian National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Unity) (RNU), as well as the [National Bolshevik Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bolshevik_Party), [Eurasian Youth Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Youth_Union), and [Cossack groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combatants_of_the_war_in_Donbas#Cossacks), formed branches to recruit volunteers for the separatists.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258][^cite_note-Yudina-261][^cite_note-washingtonp-262][^cite_note-263] A former RNU member, [Pavel Gubarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Gubarev), was founder of the Donbas People's Militia and first "governor" of the Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258][^cite_note-264] RNU is particularly linked to the [Russian Orthodox Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Army),[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] one of a number of separatist units described as "pro-Tsarist" and "extremist Orthodox" nationalists.[^cite_note-Kuzio110-265][^cite_note-Likhachev-258] Neo-Nazi unit '[Rusich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusich_Group)'[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] is part of the [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group), a Russian mercenary group in Ukraine which has been linked to far-right extremism.[^cite_note-Guardian_Wagner-266][^cite_note-267]

Some of the most influential far-right nationalists among the Russian separatists are neo-imperialists, who seek to revive the [Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire).[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] These included [Igor 'Strelkov' Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Girkin), the minister of defence of the Donetsk People's Republic, who espouses Russian neo-imperialism and ethno-nationalism.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] The [Russian Imperial Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Imperial_Movement), a [white supremacist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_supremacist) militant group,[^cite_note-Guardian_Wagner-266] has recruited thousands of volunteers to join the separatists.[^cite_note-Kuzio110-265] Some separatists have flown the black-yellow-white Russian imperial flag,[^cite_note-Likhachev-258] such as the [Sparta Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparta_Battalion). In 2014, volunteers from the [National Liberation Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Liberation_Movement_\(Russia\)) joined the Donetsk People's Republic People's Militia bearing portraits of [Tsar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsar) [Nicholas II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia).[^cite_note-Yudina-261]

Other Russian nationalist volunteers involved in separatist militias included members of the [Eurasian Youth Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Youth_Union), and of banned groups such as the [Slavic Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_Union_\(Russia\)) and [Movement Against Illegal Immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_Against_Illegal_Immigration).[^cite_note-washingtonp-262] Another Russian separatist paramilitary unit, the [Interbrigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interbrigades), is made up of activists from the [National Bolshevik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bolshevism) (Nazbol) group [Other Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Other_Russia_of_E._V._Limonov).[^cite_note-Likhachev-258]

In July 2015, the head of the Donetsk People's Republic, Alexander Zakharchenko, said at a press conference that he respected Ukraine's far-right party [Right Sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_Sector) "when they beat up the gays in Kyiv and when they tried to depose Poroshenko".[^cite_note-268]

While far-right activists played a part in the early days of the conflict, their importance was often exaggerated, and their importance on both sides of the conflict declined over time. The political climate in Donetsk further pushed far-right groups into the margins.[^cite_note-Likhachev-258]

In April 2022, news outlets noted that a video posted on Donetsk People's Republic's website showed Denis Pushilin awarding a medal to Lieutenant Roman Vorobyov ([Somalia Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somalia_Battalion)) while Vorobyov was wearing patches affiliated with [neo-Nazism](54361.md): the *[Totenkopf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totenkopf)* used by the [3rd SS Panzer Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3rd_SS_Panzer_Division_Totenkopf), and the *[valknut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valknut)*. However, the video did not show Vorobyov getting his medal when it was posted on Pushilin's website.[^cite_note-Newsweek-269][^cite_note-270]

## International status

Main article: [International recognition of the Donetsk People's Republic and the Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_Donetsk_People's_Republic_and_the_Luhansk_People's_Republic)

See also: [Luhansk People's Republic § International status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic#International_status)



[Crimea](163045.md), which Russia annexed in 2014, is shown in pink. Pink in the [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) region represents areas held by the DPR/LPR in September 2014 (cities in red)

The Donetsk People's Republic (DPR) initially sought recognition as a [sovereign state](23604120.md) following its declaration of independence in April 2014. Subsequently, the DPR willingly acceded to the Russian Federation as a [Russian federal subject](701438.md) in September–October 2022, effectively ceasing to exist as a sovereign state in any capacity and revoking its status as such in the eyes of the international community. The DPR claims [direct succession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succession_of_states) to Ukraine's Donetsk Oblast.

From 2014 to 2022, Ukraine, the United Nations, and most of the international community regarded the DPR as an illegal entity occupying a portion of Ukraine's Donetsk Oblast *(see: [International sanctions during the Russo-Ukrainian war](43449324.md))*. The Luhansk People's Republic (LPR), which had a similar backstory, was regarded in the exact same way. Crimea's status was [treated slightly differently](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_status_of_Crimea) since Russia annexed that territory immediately after its declaration of independence in March 2014.

Up until February 2022, Russia did not recognise the DPR, although it maintained informal relations with the DPR. On 21 February 2022, Russia officially recognised the DPR and the LPR at the same time,[^cite_note-TASSrussiarecognition-271] marking a major escalation in the [2021–2022 diplomatic crisis between Russia and Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine). Three days later, on 24 February 2022, Russia launched a full-scale invasion of the entire country of Ukraine, partially under the pretext of protecting the DPR and the LPR. The war had wide-reaching repercussions for Ukraine, Russia, and the international community as a whole *(see: [War crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), [Humanitarian impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_impact_of_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), [Environmental impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), [Economic impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_impact_of_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), and [Ukrainian cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_cultural_heritage_during_the_2022_Russian_invasion))*. In September 2022, Russia made moves to consolidate the territories that it had occupied in Ukraine, including [Donetsk](494889.md), [Kherson](1273370.md), [Luhansk](494904.md), and [Zaporizhzhia](494936.md) Oblasts. Russia [officially annexed these four territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) in September–October 2022.

Between February 2022 and October 2022, in addition to receiving Russian recognition, the DPR was recognised by North Korea (13 July 2022)[^cite_note-\:northkoreaposition-272] and Syria (29 June 2022).[^cite_note-\:syriaposition1-273][^cite_note-\:syriaposition2-274] This means that three United Nations member states recognised the DPR in total throughout its period of claimed independence. The DPR was also recognised by three other breakaway entities: the LPR, South Ossetia (19 June 2014),[^cite_note-SOssetiaKyivPostC-275] and Abkhazia (25 February 2022).[^cite_note-abkhazdonbas-276]

### Relations with Ukraine

The Ukrainian government passed the "[Law on the special status of Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_on_the_special_status_of_Donbas?action=edit\&redlink=1)" on 16 September 2014, which designated a special status within Ukraine on certain areas of Donetsk and Luhansk regions, in line with the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements). The status lasted for three years, and then was extended annually several times.[^cite_note-277]

In January 2015, Ukraine declared the Russia-backed [separatist](286527.md) republics in Donbas to be [terrorist](30636.md) organisations.[^cite_note-278]

### Relations with Russia before 2022

Russia has recognised identity documents, diplomas, birth, and marriage certificates and vehicle registration plates as issued by the DPR and the LPR since 18 February 2017,[^cite_note-RrsUp18217-279] enabling people living in DPR-controlled territories to travel, work, or study in Russia.[^cite_note-RrsUp18217-279] According to the decree, it was signed "to protect human rights and freedoms" in accordance with "the widely recognised principles of international humanitarian law".[^cite_note-280]

On 21 February 2022, [Russian president](239646.md) [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) signed agreements on friendship, cooperation, and assistance with DPR and the LPR, coinciding with Russia's official recognition of the two quasi-states.[^cite_note-TASSrussiarecognition-271] The Russian State Duma had approved a draft resolution appealing for him to recognise both quasi-states on 15 February.[^cite_note-281] Shortly afterwards, [Abkhazia](18933375.md) also recognized the independence of the DPR.

### Relations with extremist groups

According to the [Kharkiv Human Rights Protection Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_Human_Rights_Protection_Group), a number of European politicians from [extreme-right](221220.md) and [extreme-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-left_politics) have received all-expenses-paid trips to the Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-Coynash_2014-282]

#### Far-right



Members of the far-right group [Serbian Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Action) in the Donbas.

As well as Russian far-right groups (see *[#Right-wing nationalism](#Right-wing_nationalism)*), the DPR has cultivated relations with other European far-right groups and activists. The [Lansing Institute for Global Threats and Democracies Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lansing_Institute_for_Global_Threats_and_Democracies_Studies?action=edit\&redlink=1) acquired a memorandum of cooperation between the DPR and the far-right [Russian Imperial Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Imperial_Movement), which trains foreign volunteers; including members of the neo-Nazi [Atomwaffen Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen_Division) and [Der Dritte Weg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Dritte_Weg).[^cite_note-Partisan-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-Stanford-285][^cite_note-Lansing-286][^cite_note-287] [Anton Shekhovtsov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Shekhovtsov), an expert on far-right movements in Russia and abroad, reported in 2014 that Polish neo-fascist group "Falanga", Italian far-right group "Millennium" and French [Eurasianists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasianism) had joined the Donbas separatists.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] Members of [Serbian Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Action) have also joined the Donbas separatists.[^cite_note-291]

According to Italian newspaper *[la Repubblica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Repubblica)*, well-known Italian neo-fascist Andrea Palmeri (former member of the far-right [New Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Force_\(Italy\)) party) has been fighting for the DPR since 2014, and was hailed by DPR leader Gubarev as a "real fascist".[^cite_note-292] Other far-right activists with links to the DPR include French far-right [MEP](258909.md) Jean-Luc Schaffhauser, Italian nationalist Alessandro Musolino, German [neo-Nazi](54361.md) journalist Manuel Ochsenreiter, and Emmanuel Leroy, a far-right adviser to [Marine Le Pen](572897.md), former leader of the [National Rally](67119.md).[^cite_note-DB150514-293][^cite_note-294]

Finnish neo-Nazis have been recruited for pro-Russian forces by [local far-right pro-Russian parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_politics_in_Finland#Contemporary).[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-Conversation-296] In addition to recruiting, [Janus Putkonen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janus_Putkonen) also runs the Russian-funded DONi (Donbass International News Agency) and [MV-media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MV-media), which publish pro-Russian propaganda about the DPR.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]

#### Far-left

The DPR has also cultivated relations with various far-left groups. A small number of Spanish socialists travelled to Ukraine to fight for the separatists, with some explaining they were "repaying the favour" to Russia for the [USSR's support to Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_response_to_the_Spanish_Civil_War#Soviet_Union) during the [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War).[^cite_note-BBCextremism-299] Spanish fighters founded the 'Carlos Palomino International Brigade', which flew the flag of the [Second Spanish Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Spanish_Republic). In 2015, it reportedly had less than ten members, and was later disbanded.[^cite_note-Europapress-300]

A female member of the [Israeli Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maki_\(political_party\)) also reportedly fought for the separatists in 2015.[^cite_note-301] Other examples of far-left groups fighting for the separatists were the 'DKO' (Volunteer Communist Unit) and the Interunit, which has been inactive since 2017.[^cite_note-kcpn.info-302][^cite_note-contropiano.org-303]

The Italian communist [ska punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ska_punk) group [Banda Bassotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banda_Bassotti) has also been active in support of the DPR and has organised trips to Donetsk, one of which saw the participation of [Eleonora Forenza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleonora_Forenza), a [member of the European Parliament](258909.md) for the [Communist Refoundation Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Refoundation_Party).[^cite_note-304] [Andrej Hunko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Hunko), a member of the German parliament for the far-left party [Die Linke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Left_\(Germany\)), also travelled to Donetsk to support the separatists.[^cite_note-305]

## Economy

The republic's economy is frequently described as dependent on [contraband](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contraband) and [gunrunning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gunrunning),[^cite_note-dnrlnreb-306] with some labelling it a [mafia state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mafia_state). Joining DPR military formations or its civil services has become one of the few guarantees for a stable income in the DPR.[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198]

By late October 2014, many banks and other businesses in the DPR were shut, and people were often left without social benefits payments.[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] Sources (who declined to be identified, citing security concerns) inside the DPR administration have told [Bloomberg News](3057255.md) that Russia transfers 2.5 billion [Russian rubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_rubles) (\$37 million) for pensions every month.[^cite_note-Central_Bank_With_No_Currency-307] By mid-February 2016 Russia had sent 48 humanitarian convoys to rebel-held territory that were said to have delivered more than 58,000 tons of cargo including food, medicines, construction materials, diesel generators and fuel and lubricants.[^cite_note-308] President Poroshenko called this a "flagrant violation of international law" and Valentyn Nalyvaychenko said it was a "direct invasion".[^cite_note-ConvoyPoroshenko-309]

*[Reuters](18998750.md)* in late October 2014 reported long lines at [soup kitchens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soup_kitchens).[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] In the same month in at least one factory, workers no longer received wages, only food rations.[^cite_note-Ukraine_Oligarch_Put_on_Spot_as_Pro-Russian_Rebels_Nibble_at_His_Empire-310]

By June 2015, due to logistical and transport problems, prices in DPR-controlled territory are significantly higher than in territory controlled by Ukraine.[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198] This led to an increase of supplies (of more expensive products and those of lower quality) from Russia.[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198] Mines and heavy-industry facilities damaged by shelling were forced to close, undermining the wider chain of economic ties in the region.[^cite_note-Ukraine_Oligarch_Put_on_Spot_as_Pro-Russian_Rebels_Nibble_at_His_Empire-310] Three industrial facilities were under DPR "temporary management" by late October 2014.[^cite_note-Ukraine_Oligarch_Put_on_Spot_as_Pro-Russian_Rebels_Nibble_at_His_Empire-310] By early June 2015, 80% of companies physically located in the Donetsk People's Republic had re-registered on territory under Ukrainian control.[^cite_note-dnrlnreb-306]

The new ruling elites of the DPR have displaced the previous oligarchic structures in the region.[^cite_note-311] The new powerholders expropriated profitable businesses. For instance, Rinat Akhmetov lost control over his assets in the region after they were nationalised. Under Russia's guidance, the republic set up trade and production monopolies through which the trade in coal and steel is organised. Lacking private banks, its own currency, and direct access to the Black Sea, DPR's survival depends exclusively on Russia's economic support and trade through the common border.[^cite_note-22217theguardianbackground-312]

A DPR official often promised financial support from Russia without giving specific details.[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] Prime Minister [Aleksandr Zakharchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandr_Zakharchenko) in late October 2014 stated that "We have the [Russian Federation](25391.md)'s agreement in principle on granting us special conditions on gas (deliveries)".[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] Zakharchenko also claimed that "And, finally, we managed to link up with the financial and banking structure of the Russian Federation".[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] When *Reuters* tried to get more details from a source close to Zakharchenko the only reply was "Money likes silence".[^cite_note-RAZpamtAsc-238] Early October 2014 Zakharchenko stated, "The economy will be complete, if possible, oriented towards the Russian market. We consider Russia our strategic partner". According to Zakharchenko this would "secure our economy from impacts from outside, including from Ukraine".[^cite_note-Donetsk_Republic_PM_speaks_in_favour_of_economic_cooperation_with_Russia-313] According to Yury Makohon, from the Ukrainian National Institute for Strategic Studies, "Trade volume between Russia and Donetsk Oblast has seen a massive slump since the beginning of 2014".[^cite_note-314] Since Russia did not recognise the legal status of the self-proclaimed republic, all the trade it did with it was on the basis of Ukrainian law.[^cite_note-dnrlnreb-306]

DPR authorities have created a multi-currency zone in which both the [rouble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_ruble) (Russia's currency) and the [hryvnia](722696.md) (Ukraine's currency) can be used, and also the [Euro](9472.md) and [U.S. Dollar](18717338.md).[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198][^cite_note-Donetsk_Republic_PM_speaks_in_favour_of_economic_cooperation_with_Russia-313] Cash shortages are widespread and, due to a lack of roubles, the hryvnia is the most-used currency.[^cite_note-wrcesj15-198] According to Ukraine's security services in May 2016 alone the Russian government has passed US\$19 million in cash to fund the DPR administration as well as 35,000 blank Russian passports.[^cite_note-315]

Since late February 2015, DPR-controlled territories receive their [natural gas](22131.md) directly from Russia.[^cite_note-oswgd-316] According to Russia, Ukraine should pay for these deliveries; Ukraine claims it does not receive payments for the supplies from DPR-controlled territory.[^cite_note-oswgd-316][^cite_note-317] On 2 July 2015, Ukrainian Energy Minister [Volodymyr Demchyshyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Demchyshyn) announced that he "did not expect" that Ukraine would supply [natural gas](22131.md) to territory controlled by separatist troops in the 2015–2016 heating season.[^cite_note-nugdlr-318] Since 25 November 2015 Ukraine has halted all its imports of (and payments for) natural gas from Russia.[^cite_note-319]

The DPR set up its own [mobile network operator](481601.md) called Feniks, which was to be fully operational by the end of the summer of 2015.[^cite_note-KbFiD5615-320] On 5 February 2015, [Kyivstar](2254340.md) claimed that Feniks illegally used equipment that they officially gave up in territories controlled by pro-Russian separatists.[^cite_note-KbFiD5615-320] On 18 April 2015, Prime Minister Zakharchenko issued a decree stating that all equipment given up by Kyivstar fell under the control of the separatists in order to "meet the needs of the population in the communication services".[^cite_note-KbFiD5615-320] The [SIM cards](197489.md) of Feniks display the slogan "Connection for the victory".[^cite_note-321]

In mid-March 2017, [Ukrainian president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_president) [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) signed a temporary ban on the movement of goods to and from territory controlled by DPR and LPR. Ukraine has not bought coal from the [Donets Black Coal Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donets_Black_Coal_Basin) since then.[^cite_note-322]

[Anthracite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthracite) mines under DPR control reportedly supply coal to Poland through Russian shell companies to disguise its real origin.[^cite_note-323]

According to Ukrainian and Russian media, the coal export company [Vneshtorgservis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vneshtorgservis?action=edit\&redlink=1), owned by [Serhiy Kurchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Kurchenko), owes massive debts to coal mines located in separatist-controlled territory and other local companies.[^cite_note-324]

Sergey Zdrilyuk ("Abwehr"), former deputy of DPR militia, stated in an interview in 2020 that large-scale disassembly of mining equipment for scrap metal and other forms of [looting](209811.md) took place routinely during [Igor Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Strelkov_\(officer\))'s time as a militia commander, and that Girkin took significant amounts of money with him to Moscow. Militia groups such as "Vostok" and "Oplot" as well as various "Cossack formations", were involved in looting on systematic basis.[^cite_note-\:2-325][^cite_note-\:3-326]

## Human rights

Main article: [Humanitarian situation during the war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_situation_during_the_war_in_Donbas)

An early March 2016 [United Nations](31769.md) [OHCHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHCHR) report stated that people that lived in separatist-controlled areas were experiencing "complete absence of rule of law, reports of arbitrary detention, torture and incommunicado detention, and no access to real redress mechanisms".[^cite_note-327]

[Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) evaluates the eastern Donbas territories controlled by the DPR and LPR as "not free", scoring 4 out of 100 in its 2022 Freedom in the World index, noting issues with severe political and media repression, numerous reports of torture, and arbitrary detention.[^cite_note-328] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* noted on 17 February 2022 that "Public opposition in the DPR is virtually non-existent."[^cite_note-22217theguardianbackground-312]

### War crimes



Protest in Donetsk against [OSCE](22796.md)'s inaction in Donbas, 23 October 2021

An 18 November 2014 [United Nations](31769.md) report on [eastern Ukraine](13065940.md) stated that the DPR was in a state of "total breakdown of law and order".[^cite_note-UNREU201114-329] The report noted "cases of serious human rights abuses by the armed groups continued to be reported, including torture, arbitrary and incommunicado detention, [summary executions](1227309.md), forced labour, sexual violence, as well as the destruction and illegal seizure of property may amount to [crimes against humanity](61099.md)".[^cite_note-UNREU201114-329] The November report also stated "the HRMMU continued to receive allegations of sexual and gender-based violence in the eastern regions. In one reported incident, members of the pro-Russian [Vostok Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vostok_Battalion_\(Donbas\)) "arrested" a woman for violating a curfew and beat her with metal sticks for three hours. The woman was also raped by several pro-Russian rebels from the battalion. The report also states that the UN mission "continued to receive reports of torture and ill-treatment by the Ukrainian law enforcement agencies and volunteer battalions and by the (pro-Russian separatist) armed groups, including beating, death threats, cruel, [inhuman and degrading treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inhuman_or_degrading_treatment), and lack of access to medical assistance".[^cite_note-330] In a 15 December 2014 press conference in [Kyiv](585629.md), [UN Assistant Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Assistant_Secretary-General) for [human rights](13831.md) [Ivan Šimonović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_%C5%A0imonovi%C4%87) stated that the majority of human rights violations were committed in areas controlled by pro-Russian rebels.[^cite_note-331]

The United Nations report also accused the [Ukrainian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Army) and [Ukrainian (volunteer) territorial defence battalions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_defense_battalion_\(Ukraine\)), including the [Azov Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov_Battalion),[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] of human rights abuses such as illegal detention, torture and ill-treatment of DPR and LPR supporters, noting official denials.[^cite_note-UNREU201114-329][^cite_note-334] Amnesty International reported on 24 December 2014 that pro-government volunteer battalions were [blocking Ukrainian aid convoys](293054.md) from entering separatist-controlled territory.[^cite_note-AI24DEC-335]

On 24 July, Human Rights Watch accused the pro-Russian fighters of not taking measures to avoid encamping in densely populated civilian areas."[^cite_note-washingtonpost1-336][^cite_note-HRWunguidedrockets-337] It also accused Ukrainian government forces and [pro-government volunteer battalions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_defence_battalion_\(Ukraine\)) of [indiscriminate attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack) on civilian areas, stating that "The use of indiscriminate rockets in populated areas violates international humanitarian law, or the laws of war, and may amount to war crimes."[^cite_note-washingtonpost1-336][^cite_note-HRWunguidedrockets-337]

A report by the OHCHR [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md) that was released on 2 March 2015 described media postings and online videos which indicated that the pro-Russian armed groups of the DPR carried out "summary, extrajudicial or arbitrary executions" of captured Ukrainian soldiers. In one incident, corpses of Ukrainian servicemen were found with "their hands tied with white electrical cable" after the pro-Russian rebel groups captured [Donetsk International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_International_Airport). In January a DPR leader claimed that the rebel forces were detaining up to five "subversives" between the ages of 18 and 35 per day. A number of captured [prisoners of war](25008.md) were forced to march in Donetsk while being assaulted by rebel soldiers and onlookers. The report also said that Ukrainian law enforcement agencies had engaged in a "pattern of enforced disappearances, secret detention and ill-treatment" of people suspected of "separatism" and "terrorism".[^cite_note-OHCHR32MARCH2015-338] The report also mentions videos of members of one particular pro-Russian unit talking about running a torture facility in the basement of a Luhansk library. The head of the unit in question was the pro-Russian separatist commander Aleksandr Biednov, known as "Batman" (who was later killed) and the "head" of the torture chamber was a rebel called "Maniac" who "allegedly used a hammer to torture prisoners and surgery kit to scare and extract confessions from prisoners".[^cite_note-OHCHR32MARCH2015-338][^cite_note-339]

In September 2015, [OSCE](22796.md) published a report on the testimonies of victims held in places of illegal detention in Donbas.[^cite_note-340] In December 2015, a team led by [Małgorzata Gosiewska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma%C5%82gorzata_Gosiewska) published a comprehensive report on war crimes in Donbas.[^cite_note-341]

### Allegations of anti-semitism

Alleged members of the [Donetsk Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Republic_\(organization\)) carrying the flag of the Russian Federation,[^cite_note-usatoday0417-342] passed out a leaflet to Jews that informed all Jews over the age of 16 that they would have to report to the *Commissioner for Nationalities* in the Donetsk Regional Administration building and register their property and religion. It also claimed that Jews would be charged a \$50 'registration fee'.[^cite_note-Ynet_news-343] If they did not comply, they would have their citizenship revoked, face 'forceful expulsion' and see their assets confiscated. The leaflet stated the purpose of registration was because "Jewish community of Ukraine supported *[Bandera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stepan_Bandera)* [Junta](216815.md)," and "oppose the pro-Slavic People's Republic of Donetsk".[^cite_note-usatoday0417-342] The authenticity of the [leaflet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaflet_\(information\)) could not be independently verified.[^cite_note-GianlucaMezzofiore16April2014-344] *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)*, and *[The New Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Republic)* said the fliers were "most likely a hoax".[^cite_note-nyt-downside-345][^cite_note-hareetzfl-346][^cite_note-tnrjews-347] [France 24](1263244.md) also reported on the questionable authenticity of the leaflets.[^cite_note-franceasl-348] According to [Efraim Zuroff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efraim_Zuroff) of the [Simon Wiesenthal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center), the leaflets looked like some sort of provocation, and an attempt to paint the pro-Russian forces as [anti-semitic](1078.md).[^cite_note-349] The chief rabbi of Donetsk Pinchas Vishedski stated that the flyer was a fake meant to discredit the self-proclaimed republic,[^cite_note-tg-asf-350] and saying that anti-Semitic incidents in eastern Ukraine are "rare, unlike in Kyiv and western Ukraine"[^cite_note-351] and believes the men were 'trying to use the Jewish community in Donetsk as an instrument in the conflict;'[^cite_note-vishedski-352] however, he also called the DPR Press Secretary Aleksander Kriakov "the most famous anti-Semite in the region" and questioned DPR's decision to appoint him.[^cite_note-dailybeast17-353]

### Religion

Religion in [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) (Donetsk + Luhansk) (2016)

[^cite_note-Razumkov2016-354]

1. Not religious, [agnostics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism) and [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) (43.0%)

2. [Eastern Orthodoxy](10186.md) (28.9%)

3. Simply [Christianity](5211.md) (6.80%)

4. [Islam](6037917.md) (3.40%)

5. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism) (1.40%)

6. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (0.30%)

7. Other religions (16.1%)

At first the DPR adopted a constitution which stated that the [Russian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church) of the Moscow Patriarchate was the official religion of the self-declared state.[^cite_note-rchurch-355][^cite_note-afpchrist-356] This was changed with the promulgation of a law "on freedom of conscience and religious organisation" in November 2015, backed by three deputies professing [Rodnovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodnovery) (Slavic native faith), whose members organised the [Svarozhich Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separatist_forces_of_the_war_in_Donbas) (of the Vostok Brigade) and the Rusich Company.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] The new law caused the dissatisfaction of Metropolitan Hilarion of Donetsk and Mariupol of the Moscow Patriarchate church.[^cite_note-359]

Donetsk separatists consider Christian denominations such as the [Ukrainian Orthodox Church – Kyiv Patriarchate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Orthodox_Church_%E2%80%93_Kyiv_Patriarchate), [Ukrainian Autocephalous Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Autocephalous_Orthodox_Church), [Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Greek_Catholic_Church) and wider [Roman Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church), and [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism), as all being anti-Russian and see them as obstacles in the path of the separatist goal of uniting the region with Russia.[^cite_note-afpchrist-356]

### Romani people

Hundreds of Romani families fled Donbas in 2014.[^cite_note-360] The *News of Donbas* reported that members of the [Donbas People's Militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas_People's_Militia) engaged in assaults and robbery on the [Romani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) (also known as gypsies) population of [Sloviansk](2533448.md). The armed separatists beat women and children, looted homes, and carried off the stolen goods in trucks, according to eyewitnesses.[^cite_note-roma2-361][^cite_note-osceroma-362][^cite_note-romani1-363][^cite_note-364] Romani fled en masse to live with relatives in other parts of the country, fearing ethnic cleansing, displacement and murder. Some men who decided to remain formed militia groups to protect their families and homes.[^cite_note-romani1-363] DPR Mayor Ponomarev said the attacks were only against gypsies who were involved in drug trafficking, and that he was 'cleaning the city from drugs.'[^cite_note-365] The [US mission to the OSCE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Mission_to_the_Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe) and Ukrainian prime minister [Yatsenyuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yatsenyuk) condemned these actions.[^cite_note-roma2-361][^cite_note-osceroma-362][^cite_note-roma1-366]

### LGBT community

For broader coverage of this topic, see [LGBT people in the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_people_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).

On 8 June 2014, it was reported that armed militants from the Donetsk Republic attacked a gay club in the capital of Donetsk, injuring several. Witnesses said 20 people forced their way into the club, stealing jewellery and other valuables; the assailants fired shots in the club, and several people were hurt.[^cite_note-367] In July 2015, a DPR Ministry of Information spokesperson stated "there are no gays in Donetsk, as they all went to [Kyiv](585629.md)".[^cite_note-368] In 2015, the Deputy Minister of Political Affairs of the Donetsk People's Republic stated: "A culture of homosexuality is spreading ... This is why we must kill anyone who is involved in this."[^cite_note-369]

### Prejudice against Ukrainian speakers

On 18 April 2014, Vyacheslav Ponomarev asked local residents of [Sloviansk](2533448.md) to report all suspicious persons, especially if they were speaking Ukrainian. He also promised that the local media would publish a phone number to report them.[^cite_note-370]

An 18 November 2014 [United Nations](31769.md) report on [eastern Ukraine](13065940.md) stated that the DPR violated the rights of Ukrainian-speaking children because schools in rebel-controlled areas teach only in Russian and forbid pupils to speak Ukrainian.[^cite_note-UNREU201114-329] In its May 2014 constitution, the DPR regime declared Russian and [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) its official languages.[^cite_note-URSDDPR-107] However, in March 2020, [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) was declared to be the sole official language of the DPR.[^cite_note-2020/03/06/russkij-220]

### Abductions

Main article: [Izolyatsia prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izolyatsia_prison)

The [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists) said that separatists had seized up to ten foreign reporters during the week following the [shooting down of the Malaysian aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia_Airlines_Flight_17).[^cite_note-CNN140724-371] On 22 July 2014, armed men from the DPR abducted Ukrainian freelance journalist Anton Skiba as he arrived with a [CNN](62028.md) crew at a hotel in [Donetsk](562951.md).[^cite_note-CNN140724-371] The DPR often counters such accusations by pointing towards non-governmental organisations, such as [Amnesty International](18947898.md)'s reporting that pro-Ukrainian volunteer paramilitary battalions, such as the [Aidar Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aidar_Battalion), [Donbas Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas_Battalion), [Azov Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov_Battalion) often acted like "renegade gangs", and were implicated in torture, abductions, and summary executions.[^cite_note-AI24DEC-335][^cite_note-AMmouade-372] Amnesty International and the (OHCHR) also raised similar concerns about [Radical Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_Party_of_Oleh_Lyashko) leader and Ukrainian MP [Oleh Lyashko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Lyashko) and his militia.[^cite_note-373]

Donetsk has also observed significant rise in violent crime (homicide, rape, including underage victims) under the control of separatist forces.[^cite_note-374] In July 2015 local authorities of Druzhkovka, previously occupied by separatist forces, exposed a previous torture site in one of the town's cellars.[^cite_note-375]

On 2 June 2017 the freelance journalist [Stanislav Aseyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislav_Aseyev) was abducted. Firstly the DPR "government" denied knowing his whereabouts but on 16 July, an agent of the DPR's Ministry of State Security confirmed that Aseyev was in their custody and that he was suspected of espionage. Independent media is not allowed to report from the DPR-controlled territory.[^cite_note-376] [Amnesty International](18947898.md), the [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists), the [European Federation of Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Federation_of_Journalists), [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), the [Norwegian Helsinki Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Helsinki_Committee), the [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md), [PEN International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_International), [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) and the [United States Mission to the Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Mission_to_the_Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe) have called for the immediate release of Aseyev.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] He was released as part of a prison exchange and handed over to Ukrainian authorities on 29 December 2019.[^cite_note-383]

Sergey Zdrilyuk ("Abwehr"), former deputy of DPR militia, confirmed in 2020 that [Igor Girkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Strelkov_\(officer\)) personally executed [prisoners of war](25008.md) he considered "traitors" or "spies".[^cite_note-\:2-325][^cite_note-\:3-326] This statement was first made in Girkin's interview earlier that year, although Girkin insisted the executions were part of his "military tribunal based on laws of war". Girkin also confessed that he was involved in the murder of [Volodymyr Ivanovych Rybak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Ivanovych_Rybak), a representative of [Horlivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horlivka) who was abducted on 17 April 2014 after trying to raise a Ukrainian flag: "Naturally, Rybak, as a person who actively opposed the "militias", was an enemy in my eyes. And his death, probably, is to some extent also under my responsibility".[^cite_note-384]

### Forced Passportization

Main article: [Russification of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russification_of_Ukraine)

According to a US study, Ukrainians in occupied territories who refuse Russian passports face threats, intimidation and possible detention or deportation.[^cite_note-Sullivan_2023_a352-385][^cite_note-Conflict_Observatory_Publication_Portal_Redirection_2023_o252-386] In April 2022, Russia adopted a law that allows authorities to detain or deport residents without Russian passports. Starting July 2024, residents without Russian citizenship would be considered "foreigners" or "stateless". In June 2023, the head of the so-called "Donetsk People’s Republic" announced a planning group to study facilities for detaining residents without Russian passports for deportation.[^cite_note-Sullivan_2023_a352-385]

## Education

See also: [Education in Ukraine](5231751.md) and [Language policy in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_policy_in_Ukraine)

By the start of the 2015–2016 school-year DPR's authorities had overhauled the [curriculum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curriculum).[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387] [Ukrainian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) lessons were decreased from around eight hours a week to two hours; while the time devoted to [Russian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) and [literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_literature) lessons were increased.[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387] The history classes were changed to give greater emphasis to the history of Donbas.[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387] The grading system was changed from (Ukraine's) 12-point scheme to the five-point grading system that is also used in Russia.[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387] According to the director of a college in Donetsk "We give students the choice between the two but the Russian one is taken into greater account".[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387] School graduates will receive a Russian certificate, allowing them to enter both local universities and [institutions in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_institutions_of_higher_learning_in_Russia).[^cite_note-Rebel-held_Ukraine_education_system-387]

In April 2016 DPR authorities designed "statehood awareness lessons" were introduced in schools (in territory controlled by them).[^cite_note-388]

President Poroshenko: The war claimed its first victim in Donbas on 13 April 2014. His name was Gennady Bilichenko. He was a Ukrainian officer and part of a group that had strict orders not to respond to provocations. Back then, we entertained the illusion that war was still avoidable. That group was ambushed near the city of Slovyansk. Gennady died from gunfire. Six days later, the same Russian officers detained Volodymyr Rybak, a local politician in Horlivka who wanted only to raise the Ukrainian flag in his town. At his side was 19-year-old activist Yuriy Popravko. They tortured them, split open their stomachs and pushed them, while still alive, into the river. That is how Russia's war in Donbas started.

"Johan Backman says that he continues to help Finns who have gone to Eastern Ukraine to fight. He does not feel responsible for the people he recruits." The RIM has also provided specific paramilitary training to far-right groups in Finland. Some members of these groups have fought on Russia's side in Ukraine, while others have attempted to establish a Finnish cell of the international neo-nazi Atomwaffen Division. Police raids in 2023 also unveiled plans to assassinate the then Finnish prime minister, Sanna Marin.
